{"parentUuid":"0b97285a-2177-4ada-9d06-e5c3447dde16","isSidechain":false,"promptId":"439bc6a8-162d-420f-90a5-d2fa8da4efdc","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_016pBqkLbceyp3JwhwnWqkCN","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t---\n2\tobservation_id: sandwich-verifier-S64-BC-6\n3\ttype: sandwich-verifier-output\n4\tsession: S64\n5\ttarget: BC-6 Influence Network (G+H+I)\n6\tverdict: PASS-WITH-RESIDUE\n7\t---\n8\t\n9\t## Summary\n10\t\n11\tBC-6 Influence Network (Tracks G+H+I shipped across S46+S47+S49) is structurally sound and meets the binding contract for Phase 3 deferred-verify scope. Domain purity is clean (zero framework deps; zero Any; zero cross-BC imports), I-S1 NO-LLM-MATH is enforced (grep-clean in packages/domain/influence/services/), the LLM extractor cites D-026 substrate-boundary patterns verbatim, and the Bayesian calibration math (skeptical Beta(5,5) prior + scipy.stats.beta posterior CI + partial-as-half-success weighting) matches spec section B.3 UC-3 exactly. BR-1/2/6/7/10 invariants are all enforced at construction or repository boundary. Tests are behavior-focused (boundary cases, invariant violations, round-trip preservation) rather than implementation-coupled.\n12\t\n13\tThe verdict is PASS-WITH-RESIDUE rather than PASS because of a small but coherent set of non-blocking gaps: (1) two domain events are defined in packages/contracts/events/ but never published by the use cases that should emit them per spec section B.2; (2) ADR D-027 references a path packages/.../influence_network/ while the actual ship path is packages/.../influence/ (one-directory naming drift; not a functional bug but creates audit confusion); (3) UL glossary lists Recommendation intent as BUY/HOLD/SELL/WATCH while IMPL+spec ship STRONG_BUY/BUY/WATCH/NEUTRAL/AVOID/STRONG_AVOID; (4) one deprecated datetime.utcnow() call; (5) YouTube subtitle extraction returns a placeholder URL marker rather than actual transcript text (Q-S47-1 explicitly defers Whisper to Phase 4, so this is intentional but means UC-1 end-to-end ingestion will be a no-op until Phase 4). None of these block S65 Phase 3 progression to BC-7 PLAN; all are recoverable in Phase 4 wiring or trivial fixes.\n14\t\n15\t## Tier 1 deterministic gate\n16\t\n17\tPer main-session S64 entry (echoed):\n18\t- pytest packages/{domain,application,infrastructure}/influence -> 149/149 PASS\n19\t- mypy --explicit-package-bases packages/{domain,application,infrastructure}/influence -> no issues, 61 files\n20\t- ruff check packages/{domain,application,infrastructure}/influence -> all checks passed\n21\t\n22\tVerifier did not re-run; targeted spot-checks below confirm consistency with these gate results.\n23\t\n24\t## Tier 2 findings\n25\t\n26\t### Spec alignment\n27\t\n28\t- BR-1 (public-only sources): PASS. KOLChannelAdapter.is_public() Protocol method (packages/application/influence/ports/kol_channel_adapter_port.py:60-68) + concrete impls in YouTube/Telegram/Facebook adapters refuse private URLs. RateLimitedFetcher._fetch raises ToSBoundaryViolation on HTTP 401/403 (packages/infrastructure/influence/rate_limited_fetcher.py:188-192). robots.txt pre-flight check via stdlib urllib.robotparser in check_robots_txt (rate_limited_fetcher.py:121-137).\n29\t- BR-2 (provisional -> active at n_evaluated >= 10): PASS. Domain enforces both invariants:\n30\t  - Kol.transition_to_active(n_evaluated) raises InvariantViolation when status != PROVISIONAL or n_evaluated < 10 (packages/domain/influence/models/kol.py:67-84).\n31\t  - UpdateKolCredibilityUseCase._maybe_transition_to_active (packages/application/influence/use_cases/update_kol_credibility_use_case.py:109-119) checks kol.is_provisional AND score.n_evaluated >= 10 before calling transition.\n32\t  - Test coverage: test_provisional_to_active_transitions_at_n_10 (n=10 transitions) + test_provisional_does_not_transition_below_threshold (n=9 stays PROVISIONAL) + test_active_kol_does_not_re_transition (already-ACTIVE no-op) in test_update_kol_credibility.py:171-213.\n33\t- BR-3 (1m/3m/6m/12m outcome reviews): PASS. SqliteOutcomeScheduler.schedule_for writes 4 rows (one per ReviewWindow) per Recommendation idempotently via INSERT OR IGNORE (packages/infrastructure/influence/outcome_scheduler.py:43-75). UNIQUE constraint UNIQUE(recommendation_id, review_window) in schema (sqlite_outcome_review_repository.py:42). window_offset deterministic in domain (outcome_review.py:67-77).\n34\t- Recommendation extraction contract: PASS. Recommendation.__post_init__ (recommendation.py:92-141) enforces ALL of BR-10 (source_url, transcript_excerpt, extractor_model, extractor_version, extracted_at non-empty), BR-6 (extraction_confidence < 0.7 must have requires_manual_review=True), and the spec section B.6 <=500 char excerpt cap. SQLite schema CHECK (LENGTH(transcript_excerpt) <= 500) (sqlite_recommendation_repository.py:41). LLM extractor _hydrate_recommendation (llm_recommendation_extractor.py:363-445) clamps confidence to [0,1], skips items <0.5 per spec section B.5 rule, sets requires_manual_review=True when 0.5 <= confidence < 0.7. Recommendation ID is uuid4 generated server-side (line 422), not LLM-supplied -- clean I-S1 boundary.\n35\t\n36\t### Architecture boundaries\n37\t\n38\t- D-026 LLM Substrate Boundary: PASS. llm_recommendation_extractor.py cites all 3 patterns by name in module docstring (packages/infrastructure/influence/llm_recommendation_extractor.py:6-56):\n39\t  - Pattern 1 (Per-role override BP-S43b-1): implemented as role_model_overrides dict[str,str] field with default factory at line 217-219; extract reads model from role_model_overrides.get(extraction, ...) at line 252; extract_from_context allows gatherer to override at line 282-285.\n40\t  - Pattern 2 (Prose-tolerant JSON BP-S43b-2): 3-tier extractor _unwrap_fence (line 142-158) tries _INNER_FENCE_RE regex match first (tier 1), then _extract_first_json_object_or_array for top-level brace or bracket block (tier 2), then returns text as-is (tier 3 fallback). Faithful re-implementation of the BC-8 reference pattern, extended to also handle top-level arrays since the spec section B.5 prompt returns a JSON array (correctly noted in _extract_first_json_object_or_array docstring at line 161-166).\n41\t  - Pattern 3 (Gatherer-wired compute BP-S43b-3): KOLContentGatherer (kol_content_gatherer.py:64-167) builds ExtractionContext with all numeric/structured config fields (extractor_version, confidence_threshold, transcript_excerpt_max_chars, source_url, channel_id, published_at_iso, platform, kol_id) before LLM is invoked. Comments at line 34-38 explicitly call out that constants come from code per I-S1.\n42\t- I-S1 (No LLM Math): PASS. Grep anthropic/openai/claude_llm/llm_perspective in packages/domain/influence/services/ returns 0 (verified at S49 close per session-49.md gate). Calibration math is pure scipy.stats.beta + arithmetic in calibration_service.py:148-218. bayesian_mean = post_alpha / (post_alpha + post_beta) (line 117) is deterministic Python; ci_low/ci_high = scipy_beta.ppf(...) (lines 118-119) is deterministic library call. extraction_confidence from LLM is a meta-confidence flag (used only as a routing input to requires_manual_review), NOT a financial number. No approximately-X-percent prose patterns anywhere in BC-6.\n43\t- Domain layer purity: PASS. Grep \"from pydantic | import pydantic | from fastapi | import fastapi\" returns 0 across packages/domain/influence/ AND packages/application/influence/ AND packages/infrastructure/influence/. Pure dataclasses + StrEnum + stdlib datetime/typing throughout the domain. \": Any\" and \"cast(Any, ...)\" grep returns 0 in domain layer per I-12. The only third-party import in domain is scipy.stats.beta in calibration_service.py:37 -- this is acceptable per architecture.md (scipy is a math library, not a framework; serves the deterministic compute role; matches the precedent of numpy etc. used in BC-2 RatioService).\n44\t\n45\t### Cross-BC contracts\n46\t\n47\t- No direct cross-BC imports: PASS. Grep \"from packages.{domain,application,infrastructure}.{market_data,fundamental,company_intelligence,macro,news,crowd,analysis,portfolio}\" returns 0 in BC-6 source files. BC-6 only references its own domain VOs + ports + scipy + stdlib + httpx + tenacity (infra only).\n48\t- Cross-BC contract events defined: 2 events shipped in packages/contracts/events/: outcome_review_completed.py + credibility_score_updated.py. Both are frozen dataclass + slots, properly framework-free.\n49\t- GAP -- events defined but not published: per spec section B.2 the BC-6 use cases should emit 6 events: RecommendationExtracted, OutcomeReviewScheduled, OutcomeReviewCompleted, CredibilityScoreUpdated, HighCredibilityFlipDetected, ConfluenceDetected. Sub-plan 008-S45 deliverables list only 2 (CredibilityScoreUpdated + OutcomeReviewCompleted) which were created. But neither EvaluateOutcomeReviewUseCase nor UpdateKolCredibilityUseCase actually publishes them. Grep \"event_bus|publish.*Event\" in packages/application/influence/ returns 0. This appears to be a global Phase 2/3 deferral (BC-5 packages/application/news/ also has no event_bus wiring -- the in-process event bus port itself is not shipped yet). Recommend tracking as a Phase 4 prerequisite rather than a BC-6 fix-cycle blocker; the contract types are present so future wiring is mechanical.\n50\t\n51\t### Ubiquitous language consistency\n52\t\n53\tSpot-checked 8 terms against agent-workspace/ubiquitous-language/glossary.md:\n54\t\n55\t- KOL (glossary.md:166-168) -- PASS, definition matches IMPL.\n56\t- Channel -- not separately defined in glossary section Tier 3-4, IMPL uses Channel aggregate with Platform StrEnum (5 values). No drift, but glossary could add explicit Channel entry.\n57\t- Recommendation (KOL) (glossary.md:170-172) -- DRIFT: glossary says intent values are BUY/HOLD/SELL/WATCH, but spec section B.1 + B.5 + actual Intent enum (packages/domain/influence/value_objects/intent.py:26-31) has STRONG_BUY/BUY/WATCH/NEUTRAL/AVOID/STRONG_AVOID (6 values, no SELL or HOLD; AVOID and STRONG_AVOID are the SELL-equivalent semantics). IMPL is correct vs spec; glossary is stale. Severity LOW (non-functional; documentation update needed).\n58\t- KolStyle -- not in glossary; IMPL has 5 values FUNDAMENTAL/TECHNICAL/NARRATIVE/PUMPER/MIXED (kol_style.py) per spec section A.3 BR-8. Recommend add to glossary.\n59\t- Intent -- not defined separately; IMPL faithful to spec.\n60\t- Timeframe -- not in glossary; IMPL has 5 values INTRADAY/DAYS/WEEKS/MONTHS/LONG_TERM per spec section B.1. Faithful.\n61\t- CredibilityScore -- referenced via Calibration entry (glossary.md:35) but no dedicated entry. IMPL faithful to spec section B.1.\n62\t- OutcomeReview -- not in glossary. IMPL has ReviewWindow (4 values) + OutcomeStatus (6 values) per spec section B.1. Faithful.\n63\t\n64\tNet UL drift verdict: 1 LOW-severity drift (glossary Recommendation intent values stale) + 4 missing entries that should be added at next UL refresh. None block BC-6 ship.\n65\t\n66\t### D-027 ADR alignment\n67\t\n68\tCross-checked all 6 decisions in D-027 against actual IMPL:\n69\t\n70\t- (a) Storage substrate -- SQLite extension: PASS. 4 SQLite repos shipped in packages/infrastructure/influence/sqlite_*_repository.py. Schema mirrors spec section B.6 with the documented adaptations (TIMESTAMPTZ -> TEXT ISO8601; JSONB -> TEXT json; TEXT[] -> TEXT json-array).\n71\t- (b) Adapter pattern -- Shared Protocol + 3 concretes: PASS. KOLChannelAdapter Protocol in packages/application/influence/ports/kol_channel_adapter_port.py + 3 concretes (youtube_adapter.py, telegram_adapter.py, facebook_adapter.py) all subclass-pattern + RateLimitedFetcher shared base.\n72\t- (c) Recommendation entity persistence -- Domain aggregates + Protocol repo: PASS. Rich aggregates in packages/domain/influence/models/ with __post_init__ invariants; Protocols in packages/application/influence/ports/; SQLite concretes in infrastructure. No anemic anti-pattern.\n73\t- (d) LLM extractor substrate -- D-026 verbatim citation: PASS. Cited by name in extractor docstring; all 3 patterns implemented faithfully (per details above in Architecture boundaries).\n74\t- (e) Calibration determinism -- Pure Python Bayesian in domain service: PASS. calibration_service.py uses scipy.stats.beta only; explicit grep gate at S49 close confirms 0 LLM imports. Skeptical Beta(5,5) prior matches spec section B.3 UC-3 line 295.\n75\t- (f) Outcome scheduler -- SQLite jobs + cron runner: PASS. outcome_scheduler.py writes 4 rows on schedule_for(recommendation) + apps/cli/run_due_outcome_reviews.py is the cron entry point per spec section B.7.\n76\t\n77\tMINOR drift in ADR D-027: D-027 references package paths as packages/.../influence_network/ throughout the Decisions section. Actual ship paths use packages/.../influence/ (one-directory naming drift). Sub-plan 008-S45 also has the influence_network paths throughout. The IMPL is consistent internally and the actual ship path matches architecture.md section BC-6 (which specifies packages/domain/influence/). So the correct path is what shipped; the ADR + sub-plan are stale on naming. Severity LOW (audit-trail friction; no functional impact). Recommend an addendum or supersession note on D-027.\n78\t\n79\t### Adversarial probes\n80\t\n81\t- Probe 1 -- Vietnamese diacritic boundary in transcript_excerpt 500-char limit: PASS. Recommendation.__post_init__ uses len(self.transcript_excerpt) (Python string length is character count, not byte count for str). Test test_transcript_excerpt_at_limit_ok asserts exactly 500 chars OK with diacritic; test_transcript_excerpt_over_500_raises asserts 501 raises. Char-count semantics correct for spec section B.6 wording \"<=500 chars\". NOTE: SQLite LENGTH(transcript_excerpt) <= 500 operates on UTF-8 byte length by default for TEXT in SQLite -- so a 500-char string with multi-byte diacritics could exceed 500 bytes and fail the schema check despite passing the domain check. Quick estimate: each Vietnamese diacritic char is ~2-3 UTF-8 bytes; 500 diacritics could be 1000-1500 bytes. This is a subtle inconsistency between domain (char count) and SQLite (byte count). Severity MEDIUM if real Vietnamese excerpts approach the limit; severity LOW for typical KOL excerpts (<200 chars). Mitigation: domain truncation already at 500 chars in LLM extractor (llm_recommendation_extractor.py:411) which is char-not-byte truncation, so the gap is real but only triggers on excerpts approaching 500. Worth tracking.\n82\t- Probe 2 -- Provisional KOL transition without 10 evaluations: PASS. Triple-guarded:\n83\t  1. Domain Kol.transition_to_active(n_evaluated) raises InvariantViolation when n_evaluated < 10 (kol.py:79-83).\n84\t  2. Use case _maybe_transition_to_active returns early when score.n_evaluated < 10 (update_kol_credibility_use_case.py:117-118).\n85\t  3. Test test_provisional_does_not_transition_below_threshold asserts that with n=9 reviews, kol_repo.save_calls == [] (no save attempted).\n86\t  No bypass path identified. Even if a caller bypassed the use case and tried kol.transition_to_active(score.n_evaluated) with n<10, the domain raises. Defensive depth is good.\n87\t- Probe 3 -- Conditional INVALID handling without condition_evaluator wired: PASS. EvaluateOutcomeReviewUseCase.execute (evaluate_outcome_review_use_case.py:107-113) checks if rec.is_conditional() AND if self._condition_evaluator is None, then marks the review INVALID with reason \"Conditional recommendation but no condition_evaluator wired\" and persists. This is the safe-fail path -- conditional recs without evaluator land INVALID (excluded from Bayesian buckets per BR-7 + calibration_service _bucket excludes INVALID), NOT MISS. Excellent defensive design. The Phase 3 stub returns False-by-default per the docstring at evaluate_outcome_review_use_case.py:46-53 so unmet conditions do not pollute calibration.\n88\t- Probe 4 -- LLM extractor numeric leak boundary: PASS-WITH-NOTE. The LLM returns extraction_confidence (0-1 float) via JSON. This crosses the substrate boundary as a number. Strictly under I-S1 (LLM never returns a number as natural-language output), this is a structured tool-use number, not natural-language prose, so it fits the compute_roe(...) -> 17.83% pattern not the \"approximately 18%\" pattern. The number is then clamped to [0,1] at llm_recommendation_extractor.py:396 and used only as a routing flag (requires_manual_review = confidence < 0.7). It does NOT enter the calibration math (which uses HIT/MISS counts from deterministic OutcomeEvaluator). Verdict: BR-6 confidence is a meta-confidence not a financial number; I-S1 spirit preserved. The spec section B.5 system prompt explicitly distinguishes \"confidence in this extraction (not in the recommendation itself)\" -- design intent consistent. This is borderline but documented and structurally clean.\n89\t- Probe 5 -- datetime.utcnow() deprecated usage + tz-aware/naive mixing: NOTE.\n90\t  - sqlite_recommendation_repository.py:128 uses datetime.utcnow() (returns naive UTC datetime; deprecated since Python 3.12 -- issues DeprecationWarning). Other locations in BC-6 consistently use datetime.now(UTC). Severity LOW -- replace with datetime.now(UTC) in next surgical edit.\n91\t  - TZ consistency in OutcomeScheduler.schedule_for: scheduled_at = recommendation.published_at + window_offset(window) preserves TZ from published_at. If a Recommendation is constructed with a naive published_at (no __post_init__ enforcement of tz-awareness), the SQLite-stored scheduled_at.isoformat() will lack TZ suffix. get_due does string <= comparison on ISO timestamps -- mixing naive and tz-aware in the same query returns lexicographically-ordered results that may incorrectly include or exclude rows. Mitigation: enforce published_at.tzinfo is not None invariant in Recommendation.__post_init__ (currently only checks extracted_at is None). Severity LOW (no test exposed this; current callers are uniformly tz-aware via ChannelContent which DOES enforce future-check on tz-normalized timestamp). Worth a defensive __post_init__ add.\n92\t\n93\t## Recommendations for Phase 3 progression\n94\t\n95\tS65 PLAN BC-7 architect: PROCEED.\n96\t\n97\tBC-6 is structurally and contractually solid. The 5 residue items above are non-blocking:\n98\t- (R1) ADR D-027 path naming drift influence_network vs influence -- documentation-only fix; D-028 BC-7 architect at S65 should adopt the corrected influence-style path and reference back-corrected D-027 if needed.\n99\t- (R2) UL glossary stale on Recommendation intent values -- single-line fix at next glossary refresh.\n100\t- (R3) Domain events defined but not published -- global Phase 2/3 deferral; in-process event bus port + concrete should ship at Phase 4 SaaS-deploy boundary or earlier if cross-BC reactions become needed.\n101\t- (R4) datetime.utcnow() single-call deprecation + tz-aware/naive risk in published_at invariant -- tiny defensive hardening; can fold into next BC-6 surgical edit (no fix-cycle session needed).\n102\t- (R5) Char-vs-byte length inconsistency in transcript_excerpt 500-cap -- only triggers on excerpts >500 chars with multi-byte diacritics; track for first dogfood incident, not pre-emptive fix.\n103\t\n104\tNo CHARTER breaches. Domain purity is clean (zero framework deps; zero Any; zero cross-BC imports), I-S1 NO-LLM-MATH is enforced (grep-clean in packages/domain/influence/services/), the LLM extractor cites D-026 substrate-boundary patterns verbatim, and the Bayesian calibration math (skeptical Beta(5,5) prior + scipy.stats.beta posterior CI + partial-as-half-success weighting) matches spec section B.3 UC-3 exactly. BR-1/2/6/7/10 invariants are all enforced at construction or repository boundary. Tests are behavior-focused (boundary cases, invariant violations, round-trip preservation) rather than implementation-coupled.\n\nThe verdict is PASS-WITH-RESIDUE rather than PASS because of a small but coherent set of non-blocking gaps: (1) two domain events are defined in packages/contracts/events/ but never published by the use cases that should emit them per spec section B.2; (2) ADR D-027 references a path packages/.../influence_network/ while the actual ship path is packages/.../influence/ (one-directory naming drift; not a functional bug but creates audit confusion); (3) UL glossary lists Recommendation intent as BUY/HOLD/SELL/WATCH while IMPL+spec ship STRONG_BUY/BUY/WATCH/NEUTRAL/AVOID/STRONG_AVOID; (4) one deprecated datetime.utcnow() call; (5) YouTube subtitle extraction returns a placeholder URL marker rather than actual transcript text (Q-S47-1 explicitly defers Whisper to Phase 4, so this is intentional but means UC-1 end-to-end ingestion will be a no-op until Phase 4). None of these block S65 Phase 3 progression to BC-7 PLAN; all are recoverable in Phase 4 wiring or trivial fixes.\n\n## Tier 1 deterministic gate\n\nPer main-session S64 entry (echoed):\n- pytest packages/{domain,application,infrastructure}/influence -> 149/149 PASS\n- mypy --explicit-package-bases packages/{domain,application,infrastructure}/influence -> no issues, 61 files\n- ruff check packages/{domain,application,infrastructure}/influence -> all checks passed\n\nVerifier did not re-run; targeted spot-checks below confirm consistency with these gate results.\n\n## Tier 2 findings\n\n### Spec alignment\n\n- BR-1 (public-only sources): PASS. KOLChannelAdapter.is_public() Protocol method (packages/application/influence/ports/kol_channel_adapter_port.py:60-68) + concrete impls in YouTube/Telegram/Facebook adapters refuse private URLs. RateLimitedFetcher._fetch raises ToSBoundaryViolation on HTTP 401/403 (packages/infrastructure/influence/rate_limited_fetcher.py:188-192). robots.txt pre-flight check via stdlib urllib.robotparser in check_robots_txt (rate_limited_fetcher.py:121-137).\n- BR-2 (provisional -> active at n_evaluated >= 10): PASS. Domain enforces both invariants:\n  - Kol.transition_to_active(n_evaluated) raises InvariantViolation when status != PROVISIONAL or n_evaluated < 10 (packages/domain/influence/models/kol.py:67-84).\n  - UpdateKolCredibilityUseCase._maybe_transition_to_active (packages/application/influence/use_cases/update_kol_credibility_use_case.py:109-119) checks kol.is_provisional AND score.n_evaluated >= 10 before calling transition.\n  - Test coverage: test_provisional_to_active_transitions_at_n_10 (n=10 transitions) + test_provisional_does_not_transition_below_threshold (n=9 stays PROVISIONAL) + test_active_kol_does_not_re_transition (already-ACTIVE no-op) in test_update_kol_credibility.py:171-213.\n- BR-3 (1m/3m/6m/12m outcome reviews): PASS. SqliteOutcomeScheduler.schedule_for writes 4 rows (one per ReviewWindow) per Recommendation idempotently via INSERT OR IGNORE (packages/infrastructure/influence/outcome_scheduler.py:43-75). UNIQUE constraint UNIQUE(recommendation_id, review_window) in schema (sqlite_outcome_review_repository.py:42). window_offset deterministic in domain (outcome_review.py:67-77).\n- Recommendation extraction contract: PASS. Recommendation.__post_init__ (recommendation.py:92-141) enforces ALL of BR-10 (source_url, transcript_excerpt, extractor_model, extractor_version, extracted_at non-empty), BR-6 (extraction_confidence < 0.7 must have requires_manual_review=True), and the spec section B.6 <=500 char excerpt cap. SQLite schema CHECK (LENGTH(transcript_excerpt) <= 500) (sqlite_recommendation_repository.py:41). LLM extractor _hydrate_recommendation (llm_recommendation_extractor.py:363-445) clamps confidence to [0,1], skips items <0.5 per spec section B.5 rule, sets requires_manual_review=True when 0.5 <= confidence < 0.7. Recommendation ID is uuid4 generated server-side (line 422), not LLM-supplied -- clean I-S1 boundary.\n\n### Architecture boundaries\n\n- D-026 LLM Substrate Boundary: PASS. llm_recommendation_extractor.py cites all 3 patterns by name in module docstring (packages/infrastructure/influence/llm_recommendation_extractor.py:6-56):\n  - Pattern 1 (Per-role override BP-S43b-1): implemented as role_model_overrides dict[str,str] field with default factory at line 217-219; extract reads model from role_model_overrides.get(extraction, ...) at line 252; extract_from_context allows gatherer to override at line 282-285.\n  - Pattern 2 (Prose-tolerant JSON BP-S43b-2): 3-tier extractor _unwrap_fence (line 142-158) tries _INNER_FENCE_RE regex match first (tier 1), then _extract_first_json_object_or_array for top-level brace or bracket block (tier 2), then returns text as-is (tier 3 fallback). Faithful re-implementation of the BC-8 reference pattern, extended to also handle top-level arrays since the spec section B.5 prompt returns a JSON array (correctly noted in _extract_first_json_object_or_array docstring at line 161-166).\n  - Pattern 3 (Gatherer-wired compute BP-S43b-3): KOLContentGatherer (kol_content_gatherer.py:64-167) builds ExtractionContext with all numeric/structured config fields (extractor_version, confidence_threshold, transcript_excerpt_max_chars, source_url, channel_id, published_at_iso, platform, kol_id) before LLM is invoked. Comments at line 34-38 explicitly call out that constants come from code per I-S1.\n- I-S1 (No LLM Math): PASS. Grep anthropic/openai/claude_llm/llm_perspective in packages/domain/influence/services/ returns 0 (verified at S49 close per session-49.md gate). Calibration math is pure scipy.stats.beta + arithmetic in calibration_service.py:148-218. bayesian_mean = post_alpha / (post_alpha + post_beta) (line 117) is deterministic Python; ci_low/ci_high = scipy_beta.ppf(...) (lines 118-119) is deterministic library call. extraction_confidence from LLM is a meta-confidence flag (used only as a routing input to requires_manual_review), NOT a financial number. No approximately-X-percent prose patterns anywhere in BC-6.\n- Domain layer purity: PASS. Grep \"from pydantic | import pydantic | from fastapi | import fastapi\" returns 0 across packages/domain/influence/ AND packages/application/influence/ AND packages/infrastructure/influence/. Pure dataclasses + StrEnum + stdlib datetime/typing throughout the domain. \": Any\" and \"cast(Any, ...)\" grep returns 0 in domain layer per I-12. The only third-party import in domain is scipy.stats.beta in calibration_service.py:37 -- this is acceptable per architecture.md (scipy is a math library, not a framework; serves the deterministic compute role; matches the precedent of numpy etc. used in BC-2 RatioService).\n\n### Cross-BC contracts\n\n- No direct cross-BC imports: PASS. Grep \"from packages.{domain,application,infrastructure}.{market_data,fundamental,company_intelligence,macro,news,crowd,analysis,portfolio}\" returns 0 in BC-6 source files. BC-6 only references its own domain VOs + ports + scipy + stdlib + httpx + tenacity (infra only).\n- Cross-BC contract events defined: 2 events shipped in packages/contracts/events/: outcome_review_completed.py + credibility_score_updated.py. Both are frozen dataclass + slots, properly framework-free.\n- GAP -- events defined but not published: per spec section B.2 the BC-6 use cases should emit 6 events: RecommendationExtracted, OutcomeReviewScheduled, OutcomeReviewCompleted, CredibilityScoreUpdated, HighCredibilityFlipDetected, ConfluenceDetected. Sub-plan 008-S45 deliverables list only 2 (CredibilityScoreUpdated + OutcomeReviewCompleted) which were created. But neither EvaluateOutcomeReviewUseCase nor UpdateKolCredibilityUseCase actually publishes them. Grep \"event_bus|publish.*Event\" in packages/application/influence/ returns 0. This appears to be a global Phase 2/3 deferral (BC-5 packages/application/news/ also has no event_bus wiring -- the in-process event bus port itself is not shipped yet). Recommend tracking as a Phase 4 prerequisite rather than a BC-6 fix-cycle blocker; the contract types are present so future wiring is mechanical.\n\n### Ubiquitous language consistency\n\nSpot-checked 8 terms against agent-workspace/ubiquitous-language/glossary.md:\n\n- KOL (glossary.md:166-168) -- PASS, definition matches IMPL.\n- Channel -- not separately defined in glossary section Tier 3-4, IMPL uses Channel aggregate with Platform StrEnum (5 values). No drift, but glossary could add explicit Channel entry.\n- Recommendation (KOL) (glossary.md:170-172) -- DRIFT: glossary says intent values are BUY/HOLD/SELL/WATCH, but spec section B.1 + B.5 + actual Intent enum (packages/domain/influence/value_objects/intent.py:26-31) has STRONG_BUY/BUY/WATCH/NEUTRAL/AVOID/STRONG_AVOID (6 values, no SELL or HOLD; AVOID and STRONG_AVOID are the SELL-equivalent semantics). IMPL is correct vs spec; glossary is stale. Severity LOW (non-functional; documentation update needed).\n- KolStyle -- not in glossary; IMPL has 5 values FUNDAMENTAL/TECHNICAL/NARRATIVE/PUMPER/MIXED (kol_style.py) per spec section A.3 BR-8. Recommend add to glossary.\n- Intent -- not defined separately; IMPL faithful to spec.\n- Timeframe -- not in glossary; IMPL has 5 values INTRADAY/DAYS/WEEKS/MONTHS/LONG_TERM per spec section B.1. Faithful.\n- CredibilityScore -- referenced via Calibration entry (glossary.md:35) but no dedicated entry. IMPL faithful to spec section B.1.\n- OutcomeReview -- not in glossary. IMPL has ReviewWindow (4 values) + OutcomeStatus (6 values) per spec section B.1. Faithful.\n\nNet UL drift verdict: 1 LOW-severity drift (glossary Recommendation intent values stale) + 4 missing entries that should be added at next UL refresh. None block BC-6 ship.\n\n### D-027 ADR alignment\n\nCross-checked all 6 decisions in D-027 against actual IMPL:\n\n- (a) Storage substrate -- SQLite extension: PASS. 4 SQLite repos shipped in packages/infrastructure/influence/sqlite_*_repository.py. Schema mirrors spec section B.6 with the documented adaptations (TIMESTAMPTZ -> TEXT ISO8601; JSONB -> TEXT json; TEXT[] -> TEXT json-array).\n- (b) Adapter pattern -- Shared Protocol + 3 concretes: PASS. KOLChannelAdapter Protocol in packages/application/influence/ports/kol_channel_adapter_port.py + 3 concretes (youtube_adapter.py, telegram_adapter.py, facebook_adapter.py) all subclass-pattern + RateLimitedFetcher shared base.\n- (c) Recommendation entity persistence -- Domain aggregates + Protocol repo: PASS. Rich aggregates in packages/domain/influence/models/ with __post_init__ invariants; Protocols in packages/application/influence/ports/; SQLite concretes in infrastructure. No anemic anti-pattern.\n- (d) LLM extractor substrate -- D-026 verbatim citation: PASS. Cited by name in extractor docstring; all 3 patterns implemented faithfully (per details above in Architecture boundaries).\n- (e) Calibration determinism -- Pure Python Bayesian in domain service: PASS. calibration_service.py uses scipy.stats.beta only; explicit grep gate at S49 close confirms 0 LLM imports. Skeptical Beta(5,5) prior matches spec section B.3 UC-3 line 295.\n- (f) Outcome scheduler -- SQLite jobs + cron runner: PASS. outcome_scheduler.py writes 4 rows on schedule_for(recommendation) + apps/cli/run_due_outcome_reviews.py is the cron entry point per spec section B.7.\n\nMINOR drift in ADR D-027: D-027 references package paths as packages/.../influence_network/ throughout the Decisions section. Actual ship paths use packages/.../influence/ (one-directory naming drift). Sub-plan 008-S45 also has the influence_network paths throughout. The IMPL is consistent internally and the actual ship path matches architecture.md section BC-6 (which specifies packages/domain/influence/). So the correct path is what shipped; the ADR + sub-plan are stale on naming. Severity LOW (audit-trail friction; no functional impact). Recommend an addendum or supersession note on D-027.\n\n### Adversarial probes\n\n- Probe 1 -- Vietnamese diacritic boundary in transcript_excerpt 500-char limit: PASS. Recommendation.__post_init__ uses len(self.transcript_excerpt) (Python string length is character count, not byte count for str). Test test_transcript_excerpt_at_limit_ok asserts exactly 500 chars OK with diacritic; test_transcript_excerpt_over_500_raises asserts 501 raises. Char-count semantics correct for spec section B.6 wording \"<=500 chars\". NOTE: SQLite LENGTH(transcript_excerpt) <= 500 operates on UTF-8 byte length by default for TEXT in SQLite -- so a 500-char string with multi-byte diacritics could exceed 500 bytes and fail the schema check despite passing the domain check. Quick estimate: each Vietnamese diacritic char is ~2-3 UTF-8 bytes; 500 diacritics could be 1000-1500 bytes. This is a subtle inconsistency between domain (char count) and SQLite (byte count). Severity MEDIUM if real Vietnamese excerpts approach the limit; severity LOW for typical KOL excerpts (<200 chars). Mitigation: domain truncation already at 500 chars in LLM extractor (llm_recommendation_extractor.py:411) which is char-not-byte truncation, so the gap is real but only triggers on excerpts approaching 500. Worth tracking.\n- Probe 2 -- Provisional KOL transition without 10 evaluations: PASS. Triple-guarded:\n  1. Domain Kol.transition_to_active(n_evaluated) raises InvariantViolation when n_evaluated < 10 (kol.py:79-83).\n  2. Use case _maybe_transition_to_active returns early when score.n_evaluated < 10 (update_kol_credibility_use_case.py:117-118).\n  3. Test test_provisional_does_not_transition_below_threshold asserts that with n=9 reviews, kol_repo.save_calls == [] (no save attempted).\n  No bypass path identified. Even if a caller bypassed the use case and tried kol.transition_to_active(score.n_evaluated) with n<10, the domain raises. Defensive depth is good.\n- Probe 3 -- Conditional INVALID handling without condition_evaluator wired: PASS. EvaluateOutcomeReviewUseCase.execute (evaluate_outcome_review_use_case.py:107-113) checks if rec.is_conditional() AND if self._condition_evaluator is None, then marks the review INVALID with reason \"Conditional recommendation but no condition_evaluator wired\" and persists. This is the safe-fail path -- conditional recs without evaluator land INVALID (excluded from Bayesian buckets per BR-7 + calibration_service _bucket excludes INVALID), NOT MISS. Excellent defensive design. The Phase 3 stub returns False-by-default per the docstring at evaluate_outcome_review_use_case.py:46-53 so unmet conditions do not pollute calibration.\n- Probe 4 -- LLM extractor numeric leak boundary: PASS-WITH-NOTE. The LLM returns extraction_confidence (0-1 float) via JSON. This crosses the substrate boundary as a number. Strictly under I-S1 (LLM never returns a number as natural-language output), this is a structured tool-use number, not natural-language prose, so it fits the compute_roe(...) -> 17.83% pattern not the \"approximately 18%\" pattern. The number is then clamped to [0,1] at llm_recommendation_extractor.py:396 and used only as a routing flag (requires_manual_review = confidence < 0.7). It does NOT enter the calibration math (which uses HIT/MISS counts from deterministic OutcomeEvaluator). Verdict: BR-6 confidence is a meta-confidence not a financial number; I-S1 spirit preserved. The spec section B.5 system prompt explicitly distinguishes \"confidence in this extraction (not in the recommendation itself)\" -- design intent consistent. This is borderline but documented and structurally clean.\n- Probe 5 -- datetime.utcnow() deprecated usage + tz-aware/naive mixing: NOTE.\n  - sqlite_recommendation_repository.py:128 uses datetime.utcnow() (returns naive UTC datetime; deprecated since Python 3.12 -- issues DeprecationWarning). Other locations in BC-6 consistently use datetime.now(UTC). Severity LOW -- replace with datetime.now(UTC) in next surgical edit.\n  - TZ consistency in OutcomeScheduler.schedule_for: scheduled_at = recommendation.published_at + window_offset(window) preserves TZ from published_at. If a Recommendation is constructed with a naive published_at (no __post_init__ enforcement of tz-awareness), the SQLite-stored scheduled_at.isoformat() will lack TZ suffix. get_due does string <= comparison on ISO timestamps -- mixing naive and tz-aware in the same query returns lexicographically-ordered results that may incorrectly include or exclude rows. Mitigation: enforce published_at.tzinfo is not None invariant in Recommendation.__post_init__ (currently only checks extracted_at is None). Severity LOW (no test exposed this; current callers are uniformly tz-aware via ChannelContent which DOES enforce future-check on tz-normalized timestamp). Worth a defensive __post_init__ add.\n\n## Recommendations for Phase 3 progression\n\nS65 PLAN BC-7 architect: PROCEED.\n\nBC-6 is structurally and contractually solid. Promotion candidate: extend hook to BLOCK (not just WARN) at >2x ceiling, OR auto-archive oldest rows at WARN threshold.\n\n---\n\n### M-S64-1: Duplicate sandwich-verifier dispatch — pre-dispatch in-flight check failure (L-S49b-3 violation)\n**Date**: 2026-05-05 (S64 entry)\n**Severity**: MEDIUM (process violation; positive cross-check side effect surfaced ADDITIONAL HIGH-severity finding canonical verifier missed — net beneficial outcome but rule-compliance failure)\n\n**What happened**: At S64 entry, main session read S63 checkpoint's `in_flight_subagent_dispatch` schema which recorded `agent_id: a91164d4c20d05d63` as in-flight verifier (actually dispatched in S63 close turn, not yet returned). Main session interpreted the schema as a placeholder/scheduled-not-fired entry and dispatched a duplicate verifier `agent_id: a4345898b6912af26` against the same target (BC-6 Tracks G+H+I). Both completed independently with adversarial-different findings.\n\n**Root cause** (3-layer):\n- L1 (interpretation gap): Schema field `dispatched_at: 2026-05-05  # S64 entry; same conversational context as S63 close` was ambiguous — comment phrase \"S64 entry\" misled main session into reading this as \"to be dispatched at S64 entry\" instead of \"already dispatched at S63 close, awaiting S64 consumption\".\n- L2 (no fingerprint match check): Pre-dispatch in-flight check should have noted that observation file `agent-workspace/memory/observations/sandwich-verifier-S64-BC-6.md` did NOT exist YET → could mean either \"not dispatched\" OR \"dispatched, still running\". Main session assumed former; should have checked task-process state first (e.g., via TaskList of running agents).\n- L3 (no schema state field): `in_flight_subagent_dispatch` schema lacks an explicit `status: scheduled | in_flight | returned | consumed` field. Adding one would make the disambiguation deterministic.\n\n**Prevention rule**: Before any new sandwich-verifier dispatch, agent MUST:\n1. Read `in_flight_subagent_dispatch` schema in latest checkpoint\n2. For any entry with target overlap: check task state (TaskList for running agents; observation file mtime for completed) rather than re-dispatching\n3. If disambiguation ambiguous: ASSUME in-flight (the conservative default) and AWAIT notification rather than dispatching anew\n4. Future schema improvement: add explicit `status:` field with deterministic values\n\n**Recurrence risk**: MEDIUM. Same checkpoint-handoff pattern repeats every session; ambiguous comment phrasing could recur. Mitigation: schema field addition (proposed L-S64-1 codification deferred to S65 if needed).\n\n**Side effect (POSITIVE)**: The duplicate dispatch acted as adversarial cross-check. Companion verifier (`a4345898b6912af26`) empirically reproduced a charter-tier Telegram BR-1 fail-open vulnerability (`telegram_adapter.py:114-116` — `is_public(\"https://t.me/+invite\")` returns True without bot_token) that canonical verifier missed entirely. Patched THIS TURN.\n\n**Cost**: ~75K main-session tokens (companion verifier consumed) + duplicate observation work. Main session interpreted the schema as a placeholder/scheduled-not-fired entry and dispatched a duplicate verifier `agent_id: a4345898b6912af26` against the same target (BC-6 Tracks G+H+I). Both completed independently with adversarial-different findings.\n\n**Root cause** (3-layer):\n- L1 (interpretation gap): Schema field `dispatched_at: 2026-05-05  # S64 entry; same conversational context as S63 close` was ambiguous — comment phrase \"S64 entry\" misled main session into reading this as \"to be dispatched at S64 entry\" instead of \"already dispatched at S63 close, awaiting S64 consumption\".\n- L2 (no fingerprint match check): Pre-dispatch in-flight check should have noted that observation file `agent-workspace/memory/observations/sandwich-verifier-S64-BC-6.md` did NOT exist YET → could mean either \"not dispatched\" OR \"dispatched, still running\". Main session assumed former; should have checked task-process state first (e.g., via TaskList of running agents).\n- L3 (no schema state field): `in_flight_subagent_dispatch` schema lacks an explicit `status: scheduled | in_flight | returned | consumed` field. Adding one would make the disambiguation deterministic.\n\n**Prevention rule**: Before any new sandwich-verifier dispatch, agent MUST:\n1. Read `in_flight_subagent_dispatch` schema in latest checkpoint\n2. For any entry with target overlap: check task state (TaskList for running agents; observation file mtime for completed) rather than re-dispatching\n3. If disambiguation ambiguous: ASSUME in-flight (the conservative default) and AWAIT notification rather than dispatching anew\n4. Future schema improvement: add explicit `status:` field with deterministic values\n\n**Recurrence risk**: MEDIUM. Same checkpoint-handoff pattern repeats every session; ambiguous comment phrasing could recur. Mitigation: schema field addition (proposed L-S64-1 codification deferred to S65 if needed).\n\n**Side effect (POSITIVE)**: The duplicate dispatch acted as adversarial cross-check. Companion verifier (`a4345898b6912af26`) empirically reproduced a charter-tier Telegram BR-1 fail-open vulnerability (`telegram_adapter.py:114-116` — `is_public(\"https://t.me/+invite\")` returns True without bot_token) that canonical verifier missed entirely. Patched THIS TURN.\n\n**Cost**: ~75K main-session tokens (companion verifier consumed) + duplicate observation work. {"parentUuid":"2ff77bbe-4d95-4d19-ae2d-82bd5e5ee599","isSidechain":false,"message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-7","id":"msg_01J38kCxu54DB7a29JM2LUDm","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01TUpwhnkkLoEq9iu9otypnU","name":"Edit","input":{"replace_all":false,"file_path":"C:\\htdocs\\stockforge\\packages\\infrastructure\\influence\\telegram_adapter.py","old_string":"    def is_public(self, channel_url: str) -> bool:\n        \"\"\"Return True if the Telegram channel is a public broadcast channel.\n\n        Public channels have a @username and type=\"channel\" in getChat response.\n        Private supergroups, private groups, and DMs raise ToSBoundaryViolation.\n        \"\"\"\n        if not channel_url:\n            return False\n\n        chat_id = self._extract_chat_id(channel_url)\n        if not chat_id:\n            return False\n\n        # If we can't call API (no token + no mock), optimistically allow @username format\n        if self.mock_api_caller is None and not self.bot_token:\n            return channel_url.startswith(\"https://t.me/\") or chat_id.startswith(\"@\")","new_string":"    def is_public(self, channel_url: str) -> bool:\n        \"\"\"Return True if the Telegram channel is a public broadcast channel.\n\n        Public channels have a @username and type=\"channel\" in getChat response.\n        Private supergroups, private groups, and DMs raise ToSBoundaryViolation.\n\n        BR-1 fail-open hardening (M-S64-1): even without bot_token, refuse\n        URLs matching private-invite patterns (`t.me/+...`, `t.me/joinchat/...`,\n        `t.me/c/...`) to prevent autonomous-pipeline accidentally treating\n        private-invite links as public per BR-1.\n        \"\"\"\n        if not channel_url:\n            return False\n\n        # BR-1 hard refusals (apply BEFORE _extract_chat_id strips the path):\n        # private invite patterns must never pass `is_public`, even when no\n        # bot_token is wired — the BR-1 gatekeeper is the only line of defense.\n        if (\n            channel_url.startswith(\"https://t.me/+\")\n            or channel_url.startswith(\"https://t.me/joinchat/\")\n            or channel_url.startswith(\"https://t.me/c/\")\n        ):\n            return False\n\n        chat_id = self._extract_chat_id(channel_url)\n        if not chat_id:\n            return False\n\n        # If we can't call API (no token + no mock), optimistically allow only\n        # canonical public-channel patterns: t.me/<plain_slug> or @<plain_slug>.\n        # Private-invite patterns already refused above; here we additionally\n        # require the slug to look like a username (alphanumeric + underscore).\n        if self.mock_api_caller is None and not self.bot_token:\n            slug = chat_id.lstrip(\"@\")\n            if not slug or not slug.replace(\"_\", \"\").isalnum():\n                return False\n            return channel_url.startswith(\"https://t.me/\") or chat_id.startswith(\"@\")"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":"tool_use","stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1882,"cache_read_input_tokens":161450,"output_tokens":1085,"server_tool_use":{"web_search_requests":0,"web_fetch_requests":0},"service_tier":"standard","cache_creation":{"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":1882,"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0},"inference_geo":"","iterations":[{"input_tokens":1,"output_tokens":1085,"cache_read_input_tokens":161450,"cache_creation_input_tokens":1882,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":1882},"type":"message"}],"speed":"standard"},"diagnostics":null},"requestId":"req_011Cajo98cejJhVLNXCA2gW9","type":"assistant","uuid":"df442528-d3ff-47d0-856c-f65e8a474a98","timestamp":"2026-05-05T17:12:34.578Z","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"C:\\htdocs\\stockforge","sessionId":"2df6e9af-7f18-4b46-89a2-51bdcedef08e","version":"2.1.124","gitBranch":"main"}

{"parentUuid":"df442528-d3ff-47d0-856c-f65e8a474a98","isSidechain":false,"promptId":"439bc6a8-162d-420f-90a5-d2fa8da4efdc","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01TUpwhnkkLoEq9iu9otypnU","type":"tool_result","content":"The file C:\\htdocs\\stockforge\\packages\\infrastructure\\influence\\telegram_adapter.py has been updated successfully."}]},"uuid":"bee9ab86-f6c1-4aec-ba67-48a7b10ab5ec","timestamp":"2026-05-05T17:12:35.137Z","toolUseResult":{"filePath":"C:\\htdocs\\stockforge\\packages\\infrastructure\\influence\\telegram_adapter.py","oldString":"    def is_public(self, channel_url: str) -> bool:\n        \"\"\"Return True if the Telegram channel is a public broadcast channel.\n\n        Public channels have a @username and type=\"channel\" in getChat response.\n        Private supergroups, private groups, and DMs raise ToSBoundaryViolation.\n        \"\"\"\n        if not channel_url:\n            return False\n\n        chat_id = self._extract_chat_id(channel_url)\n        if not chat_id:\n            return False\n\n        # If we can't call API (no token + no mock), optimistically allow @username format\n        if self.mock_api_caller is None and not self.bot_token:\n            return channel_url.startswith(\"https://t.me/\") or chat_id.startswith(\"@\")","newString":"    def is_public(self, channel_url: str) -> bool:\n        \"\"\"Return True if the Telegram channel is a public broadcast channel.\n\n        Public channels have a @username and type=\"channel\" in getChat response.\n        Private supergroups, private groups, and DMs raise ToSBoundaryViolation.\n\n        BR-1 fail-open hardening (M-S64-1): even without bot_token, refuse\n        URLs matching private-invite patterns (`t.me/+...`, `t.me/joinchat/...`,\n        `t.me/c/...`) to prevent autonomous-pipeline accidentally treating\n        private-invite links as public per BR-1.\n        \"\"\"\n        if not channel_url:\n            return False\n\n        # BR-1 hard refusals (apply BEFORE _extract_chat_id strips the path):\n        # private invite patterns must never pass `is_public`, even when no\n        # bot_token is wired — the BR-1 gatekeeper is the only line of defense.\n        if (\n            channel_url.startswith(\"https://t.me/+\")\n            or channel_url.startswith(\"https://t.me/joinchat/\")\n            or channel_url.startswith(\"https://t.me/c/\")\n        ):\n            return False\n\n        chat_id = self._extract_chat_id(channel_url)\n        if not chat_id:\n            return False\n\n        # If we can't call API (no token + no mock), optimistically allow only\n        # canonical public-channel patterns: t.me/<plain_slug> or @<plain_slug>.\n        # Private-invite patterns already refused above; here we additionally\n        # require the slug to look like a username (alphanumeric + underscore).\n        if self.mock_api_caller is None and not self.bot_token:\n            slug = chat_id.lstrip(\"@\")\n            if not slug or not slug.replace(\"_\", \"\").isalnum():\n                return False\n            return channel_url.startswith(\"https://t.me/\") or chat_id.startswith(\"@\")","originalFile":"\"\"\"TelegramAdapter — BC-6 KOL channel adapter for Telegram public broadcast channels.\n\nFetches messages from public Telegram broadcast channels via the Bot API.\nOnly PUBLIC broadcast channels are supported (BR-1). Private groups, supergroups\nrequiring invite links, and direct messages are refused.\n\nRate limit: ~1 req/s via Bot API (per platform rate-limit matrix).\nCadence: every 1h per spec § B.7.\n\nChannel ID formats (Q-S46-3 IMPL decision: support both; normalise to numeric):\n- @channel_name format → API resolves to numeric\n- -1001234567890 format → used directly\n\nBot API endpoint: GET /bot{TOKEN}/getUpdates or /bot{TOKEN}/getMessages\nFor public channels: use getChat to verify public status, then forwardMessages.\n\nBR-1 enforcement: `is_public` calls getChat API to verify channel type.\nPrivate supergroups return type=\"supergroup\"; public channels return type=\"channel\".\n\nSource: specs/tier2-feature/002-influence-network-tracking.md § B.4 + § B.7.\nADR: agent-workspace/memory/decisions/027-S45-BC-6-architecture-influence-network.md (D-027 § b).\nSkill: .claude/skills/crawler-reliability/SKILL.md.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport hashlib\nimport json\nimport logging\nfrom collections.abc import Callable\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass, field\nfrom datetime import UTC, datetime\n\nfrom packages.application.influence.ports.kol_channel_adapter_port import (\n    ToSBoundaryViolation,\n)\nfrom packages.domain.influence.models.channel import Channel\nfrom packages.domain.influence.models.channel_content import ChannelContent\nfrom packages.infrastructure.influence.rate_limited_fetcher import RateLimitedFetcher\n\n__all__ = [\"TelegramAdapter\"]\n\nlogger = logging.getLogger(__name__)\n\n_TELEGRAM_API_BASE = \"https://api.telegram.org\"\n_RATE_LIMIT_SECONDS = 1.0  # Bot API allows ~1 req/s per channel\n_MAX_MESSAGES_PER_FETCH = 50  # Conservative; daily cadence expects few messages\n\n\n@dataclass\nclass TelegramAdapter:\n    \"\"\"Telegram public broadcast channel adapter.\n\n    Args:\n        bot_token:  Telegram Bot API token. Required for API calls.\n                    Tests inject None and provide a mock_api_caller.\n        mock_api_caller: Callable(url) -> dict for test injection.\n        clock:      Callable() -> datetime for deterministic test time.\n        sleeper:    Callable(float) -> None for test injection.\n    \"\"\"\n\n    bot_token: str | None = None\n    mock_api_caller: Callable[[str, dict[str, object]], dict[str, object]] | None = None\n    clock: Callable[[], datetime] = field(default_factory=lambda: lambda: datetime.now(UTC))\n    sleeper: Callable[[float], None] = field(\n        default_factory=lambda: __import__(\"time\").sleep\n    )\n    _fetcher: RateLimitedFetcher = field(init=False, repr=False)\n\n    def __post_init__(self) -> None:\n        self._fetcher = RateLimitedFetcher(\n            platform=\"telegram\",\n            rate_limit_seconds=_RATE_LIMIT_SECONDS,\n            sleeper=self.sleeper,\n            clock=self.clock,\n        )\n\n    def _api_url(self, method: str) -> str:\n        return f\"{_TELEGRAM_API_BASE}/bot{self.bot_token}/{method}\"\n\n    def _call_api(\n        self, method: str, params: dict[str, object] | None = None\n    ) -> dict[str, object]:\n        \"\"\"Call Telegram Bot API. Uses mock_api_caller in tests.\"\"\"\n        if self.mock_api_caller is not None:\n            return self.mock_api_caller(method, params or {})\n        if not self.bot_token:\n            raise RuntimeError(\"TelegramAdapter requires bot_token for live calls\")\n        url = self._api_url(method)\n        if params:\n            import urllib.parse\n            url = f\"{url}?{urllib.parse.urlencode(params)}\"\n        result = self._fetcher._fetch(url)\n        try:\n            data: dict[str, object] = json.loads(result.content)\n            return data\n        except json.JSONDecodeError as exc:\n            logger.warning(\"Telegram API JSON parse error for %s: %s\", method, exc)\n            return {}\n\n    def is_public(self, channel_url: str) -> bool:\n        \"\"\"Return True if the Telegram channel is a public broadcast channel.\n\n        Public channels have a @username and type=\"channel\" in getChat response.\n        Private supergroups, private groups, and DMs raise ToSBoundaryViolation.\n        \"\"\"\n        if not channel_url:\n            return False\n\n        chat_id = self._extract_chat_id(channel_url)\n        if not chat_id:\n            return False\n\n        # If we can't call API (no token + no mock), optimistically allow @username format\n        if self.mock_api_caller is None and not self.bot_token:\n            return channel_url.startswith(\"https://t.me/\") or chat_id.startswith(\"@\")\n\n        response = self._call_api(\"getChat\", {\"chat_id\": chat_id})\n        result = response.get(\"result\")\n        result_dict: dict[str, object] = result if isinstance(result, dict) else {}\n        chat_type = result_dict.get(\"type\", \"\")\n        if chat_type != \"channel\":\n            raise ToSBoundaryViolation(\n                f\"Telegram chat {chat_id!r} has type={chat_type!r}; \"\n                \"only public broadcast channels (type='channel') allowed per BR-1\"\n            )\n        # Verify it has a username (truly public)\n        username = result_dict.get(\"username\")\n        if not username:\n            raise ToSBoundaryViolation(\n                f\"Telegram channel {chat_id!r} has no public @username — \"\n                \"likely private channel; refusing per BR-1\"\n            )\n        return True\n\n    def respect_rate_limit(self) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Delegate to shared fetcher rate limiter.\"\"\"\n        self._fetcher.respect_rate_limit()\n\n    def fetch_new_content(\n        self,\n        channel: Channel,\n        since: datetime,\n    ) -> list[ChannelContent]:\n        \"\"\"Fetch messages from a public Telegram channel published after `since`.\n\n        Uses Bot API getUpdates or channel history endpoint.\n        Returns ChannelContent with raw_text = message text.\n        source_url = message link (t.me/channel/message_id format).\n\n        Q-S46-3: supports both @channel_name and numeric chat ID formats.\n        \"\"\"\n        chat_id = self._extract_chat_id(channel.url)\n        if not chat_id:\n            logger.warning(\n                \"Cannot extract chat_id from channel URL %s\", channel.url\n            )\n            return []\n\n        # Verify public access before fetching\n        if not self.is_public(channel.url):\n            raise ToSBoundaryViolation(\n                f\"Channel {channel.channel_id} is not a public Telegram broadcast channel\"\n            )\n\n        response = self._call_api(\n            \"getUpdates\",\n            {\n                \"chat_id\": chat_id,\n                \"limit\": _MAX_MESSAGES_PER_FETCH,\n                \"allowed_updates\": [\"channel_post\"],\n            },\n        )\n\n        updates = response.get(\"result\", [])\n        if not isinstance(updates, list):\n            updates = []\n\n        results: list[ChannelContent] = []\n        for update in updates:\n            post = update.get(\"channel_post\") or update.get(\"message\")\n            if not post:\n                continue\n\n            # Convert Unix timestamp to UTC datetime\n            date_unix = post.get(\"date\", 0)\n            published_at = datetime.fromtimestamp(date_unix, tz=UTC)\n            if published_at <= since:\n                continue\n\n            text = post.get(\"text\") or post.get(\"caption\") or \"\"\n            message_id = post.get(\"message_id\", \"\")\n            chat_username = post.get(\"chat\", {}).get(\"username\", \"\")\n\n            # Construct message link (source_url for provenance BR-1)\n            if chat_username:\n                source_url = f\"https://t.me/{chat_username}/{message_id}\"\n            else:\n                source_url = f\"{channel.url}/{message_id}\"\n\n            content_id = self._make_content_id(source_url)\n            content = ChannelContent(\n                content_id=content_id,\n                channel_id=channel.channel_id,\n                source_url=source_url,\n                published_at=published_at,\n                raw_text=text,\n                fetched_at=self.clock(),\n            )\n            results.append(content)\n            logger.info(\n                \"telegram_adapter fetched message channel=%s url=%s text_len=%d\",\n                channel.channel_id,\n                source_url,\n                len(text),\n            )\n\n        return results\n\n    @staticmethod\n    def _extract_chat_id(channel_url: str) -> str:\n        \"\"\"Extract chat ID or @username from channel URL.\n\n        Supports:\n        - https://t.me/channel_name → @channel_name\n        - @channel_name → @channel_name (passthrough)\n        - -1001234567890 → -1001234567890 (numeric passthrough)\n        \"\"\"\n        if not channel_url:\n            return \"\"\n        if channel_url.startswith(\"-\") and channel_url[1:].isdigit():\n            return channel_url  # numeric ID\n        if channel_url.startswith(\"@\"):\n            return channel_url  # @username passthrough\n        # Try to extract from t.me URL\n        if \"t.me/\" in channel_url:\n            parts = channel_url.split(\"t.me/\")\n            if len(parts) > 1:\n                slug = parts[1].split(\"/\")[0].strip()\n                if slug:\n                    return f\"@{slug}\"\n        return channel_url\n\n    @staticmethod\n    def _make_content_id(source_url: str) -> str:\n        \"\"\"Deterministic content ID from source URL.\"\"\"\n        return hashlib.sha256(source_url.encode(\"utf-8\")).hexdigest()[:16]\n","structuredPatch":[{"oldStart":103,"oldLines":16,"newStart":103,"newLines":37,"lines":[" ","         Public channels have a @username and type=\"channel\" in getChat response.","         Private supergroups, private groups, and DMs raise ToSBoundaryViolation.","+","+        BR-1 fail-open hardening (M-S64-1): even without bot_token, refuse","+        URLs matching private-invite patterns (`t.me/+...`, `t.me/joinchat/...`,","+        `t.me/c/...`) to prevent autonomous-pipeline accidentally treating","+        private-invite links as public per BR-1.","         \"\"\"","         if not channel_url:","             return False"," ","+        # BR-1 hard refusals (apply BEFORE _extract_chat_id strips the path):","+        # private invite patterns must never pass `is_public`, even when no","+        # bot_token is wired — the BR-1 gatekeeper is the only line of defense.","+        if (","+            channel_url.startswith(\"https://t.me/+\")","+            or channel_url.startswith(\"https://t.me/joinchat/\")","+            or channel_url.startswith(\"https://t.me/c/\")","+        ):","+            return False","+","         chat_id = self._extract_chat_id(channel_url)","         if not chat_id:","             return False"," ","-        # If we can't call API (no token + no mock), optimistically allow @username format","+        # If we can't call API (no token + no mock), optimistically allow only","+        # canonical public-channel patterns: t.me/<plain_slug> or @<plain_slug>.","+        # Private-invite patterns already refused above; here we additionally","+        # require the slug to look like a username (alphanumeric + underscore).","         if self.mock_api_caller is None and not self.bot_token:","+            slug = chat_id.lstrip(\"@\")","+            if not slug or not slug.replace(\"_\", \"\").isalnum():","+                return False","             return channel_url.startswith(\"https://t.me/\") or chat_id.startswith(\"@\")"," ","         response = self._call_api(\"getChat\", {\"chat_id\": chat_id})"]}],"userModified":false,"replaceAll":false},"sourceToolAssistantUUID":"df442528-d3ff-47d0-856c-f65e8a474a98","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"C:\\htdocs\\stockforge","sessionId":"2df6e9af-7f18-4b46-89a2-51bdcedef08e","version":"2.1.124","gitBranch":"main"}

{"parentUuid":"55953fb4-9c94-4ff1-979a-5a2b13d488ac","isSidechain":false,"promptId":"439bc6a8-162d-420f-90a5-d2fa8da4efdc","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01R27oztwgWtTgvTqTVMSAst","type":"tool_result","content":"The file C:\\htdocs\\stockforge\\packages\\infrastructure\\influence\\sqlite_recommendation_repository.py has been updated successfully."}]},"uuid":"b7750c4a-a75d-4fec-b416-bd0d35c4683f","timestamp":"2026-05-05T17:12:41.203Z","toolUseResult":{"filePath":"C:\\htdocs\\stockforge\\packages\\infrastructure\\influence\\sqlite_recommendation_repository.py","oldString":"from datetime import datetime, timedelta","newString":"from datetime import UTC, datetime, timedelta","originalFile":"\"\"\"SqliteRecommendationRepository — SQLite-backed Recommendation persistence.\n\nSchema follows spec § B.6 adapted for SQLite (JSONB → TEXT json; TIMESTAMPTZ →\nTEXT ISO8601). `find_recent_for_kol_ticker` powers UC-1 flip detection;\n`find_recent` powers confluence + dashboards. Both apply the BR-6 confidence\nfloor (>=0.7) at the boundary so callers never have to re-filter.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport json\nimport sqlite3\nfrom contextlib import closing\nfrom datetime import datetime, timedelta\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nfrom packages.domain.influence.models.recommendation import Recommendation\nfrom packages.domain.influence.value_objects.channel_id import ChannelId\nfrom packages.domain.influence.value_objects.intent import Intent\nfrom packages.domain.influence.value_objects.kol_id import    def test_is_public_private_supergroup_raises_tos_violation(self) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Mocked getChat returns type=supergroup — raises ToSBoundaryViolation.\"\"\"\n\n        def mock_api(method: str, _params: dict[str, object]) -> dict[str, object]:\n            if method == \"getChat\":\n                return {\"result\": {\"type\": \"supergroup\"}}\n            return {}\n\n        adapter = TelegramAdapter(\n            mock_api_caller=mock_api,\n            clock=_frozen_clock(),\n            sleeper=lambda _s: None,\n        )\n        with pytest.raises(ToSBoundaryViolation, match=\"supergroup\"):\n            adapter.is_public(\"https://t.me/private_group\")","new_string":"    def test_is_public_private_supergroup_raises_tos_violation(self) -> None:\n        \"\"\"Mocked getChat returns type=supergroup — raises ToSBoundaryViolation.\"\"\"\n\n        def mock_api(method: str, _params: dict[str, object]) -> dict[str, object]:\n            if method == \"getChat\":\n                return {\"result\": {\"type\": \"supergroup\"}}\n            return {}\n\n        adapter = TelegramAdapter(\n            mock_api_caller=mock_api,\n            clock=_frozen_clock(),\n            sleeper=lambda _s: None,\n        )\n        with pytest.raises(ToSBoundaryViolation, match=\"supergroup\"):\n            adapter.is_public(\"https://t.me/private_group\")\n\n    def test_is_public_no_token_no_mock_refuses_private_invite_patterns(self) -> None:\n        \"\"\"BR-1 fail-open hardening (M-S64-1): even without bot_token + mock,\n        adapter must refuse t.me/+invite, t.me/joinchat/, t.me/c/ patterns\n        and accept only canonical public @username/t.me/<slug> URLs.\n        \"\"\"\n        adapter = TelegramAdapter(\n            bot_token=None,\n            mock_api_caller=None,\n            clock=_frozen_clock(),\n            sleeper=lambda _s: None,\n        )\n        # Refuse: private invite patterns\n        assert adapter.is_public(\"https://t.me/+private_invite_link\") is False\n        assert adapter.is_public(\"https://t.me/joinchat/abc123\") is False\n        assert adapter.is_public(\"https://t.me/c/1234567890\") is False\n        # Refuse: empty / non-alphanumeric slug\n        assert adapter.is_public(\"\") is False\n        assert getChat returns type=supergroup — raises ToSBoundaryViolation.\"\"\"\n\n        def mock_api(method: str, _params: dict[str, object]) -> dict[str, object]:\n            if method == \"getChat\":\n                return {\"result\": {\"type\": \"supergroup\"}}\n            return {}\n\n        adapter = TelegramAdapter(\n            mock_api_caller=mock_api,\n            clock=_frozen_clock(),\n            sleeper=lambda _s: None,\n        )\n        with pytest.raises(ToSBoundaryViolation, match=\"supergroup\"):\n            adapter.is_public(\"https://t.me/private_group\")\n\n    def test_is_public_no_token_no_mock_refuses_private_invite_patterns(self) -> None:\n        \"\"\"BR-1 fail-open hardening (M-S64-1): even without bot_token + mock,\n        adapter must refuse t.me/+invite, t.me/joinchat/, t.me/c/ patterns\n        and accept only canonical public @username/t.me/<slug> URLs.\n        \"\"\"\n        adapter = TelegramAdapter(\n            bot_token=None,\n            mock_api_caller=None,\n            clock=_frozen_clock(),\n            sleeper=lambda _s: None,\n        )\n        # Refuse: private invite patterns\n        assert adapter.is_public(\"https://t.me/+private_invite_link\") is False\n        assert adapter.is_public(\"https://t.me/joinchat/abc123\") is False\n        assert adapter.is_public(\"https://t.me/c/1234567890\") is False\n        # Refuse: empty / non-alphanumeric slug\n        assert adapter.is_public(\"\") is False\n        assert adapter.is_public(\"https://t.me/\") is False\n        assert adapter.is_public(\"https://t.me/!@#$%\") is False\n        # Accept: canonical public channel patterns\n        assert adapter.is_public(\"https://t.me/stocktest\") is True\n        assert adapter.is_public(\"@stocktest\") is True\n        assert adapter.is_public(\"https://t.me/vn_stock_news\") is True  # underscore
